        Idea:
Refactor pinned shot groups in the video editor to be first-class timeline entities, eliminating an entire class of "shot group disconnects" bugs that come from fragile parallel cohesion logic spread across drag, resize, sync, and render code paths.

## Why

The video editor's pinned shot groups (the colored bordered boxes that visually group N timeline clips under a single shot label) are currently a "soft" abstraction layered on top of flat `row.actions` arrays. The shot group is just a `{ shotId, trackId, clipIds: string[] }` record stored in `data.config.pinnedShotGroups`. Cohesion (the property that all the group's clips stay contiguous on the timeline) is enforced by SEPARATE bespoke logic in every code path that mutates clip positions:

- Drag: `useClipDrag` does "deferred expansion" — when the user grabs one clip in a group, it pulls the group's other clips into the drag set. Then `multi-drag-utils.applyMultiDragMoves` calls `resolveGroupOverlaps` which treats moved clips as one shifted group.
- Resize: `TimelineCanvas` resize handlers compute a `getShotGroupAdjacent` neighbor and write a paired `ResizeOverride` so the immediate neighbor follows the dragged edge.
- Sync: `usePinnedGroupSync` runs on a debounce after the underlying Shot's `images[]` changes; it rebuilds the group's clips one-by-one and shifts subsequent clips on the row.
- Track move / cross-track drag: updates `pinnedShotGroups[].trackId` via `updatePinnedShotGroupTrackIds`.
- Delete / unpin / "deconstruct" / "delete shot": each touches `pinnedShotGroups` differently.

There's no shared "the group is the unit of editing" primitive. Each path reinvents cohesion. Bugs keep emerging in different paths with the same user-visible symptom: "the shot disconnects."

On top of that, the visual contiguity check in `TimelineCanvas.tsx` (`positionedShotGroups`, around lines 446-499) iterates `group.clipIds` IN ARRAY ORDER and checks `current.start < previous.start || current.start - previous.end > snapThreshold`. **This assumes `group.clipIds` is in left-to-right spatial order, but nothing actively maintains that invariant.** `pinGroup` stores `[...selectedClipIds]` (Set iteration order, i.e. click order), and `usePinnedGroupSync` rebuilds in `desiredImages` order from the database. Once array order disagrees with spatial order by even one position, the group's border vanishes — user reports "disconnected."

We just shipped two patches for this in the drag and resize paths. The patches work for their cases but they don't address the root cause: the shot group is not a real entity in the editing model.

## Goal

Make a pinned shot group a FIRST-CLASS timeline entity. Conceptually:

- The shot group has its own bounds (`start`, `end`) on the row.
- It contains child clips with their own positions inside that bound (or relative offsets).
- Drag, resize, selection, and serialization all see the group as ONE thing by default.
- The contiguity invariant is enforced BY CONSTRUCTION, not by validation. Children of a group can never become non-contiguous because they live inside a single owning entity that lays them out.
- Operations on individual clips inside a group are explicit, intentional, and rare (the per-clip context menu is already locked down to asset-state actions only when a clip is in a pinned group, per a recent change).

## Hard requirements

1. **Eliminate the "shot disconnects" bug class.** After this refactor, no drag, resize, sync, or other normal interaction can make a pinned shot group's children become non-contiguous. The only way to break a group is the explicit "Deconstruct shot" or "Delete shot" actions on the shot label menu.

2. **No regression in user-facing behavior** for the supported flows:
   - Pinning a group from a selection.
   - Dragging a group on its row (snaps to bounds, snaps near siblings, can hit timeline start at t=0).
   - Dragging a group cross-track (entire group moves to the new row).
   - Resizing a group's outer left/right edge (stretches/shrinks the group, content reflows).
   - Resizing an interior boundary inside a group (between child clips) — re-evaluate whether this is even meaningful in the new model. If kept, must preserve cohesion.
   - The shot label menu actions (Deconstruct, Delete, Switch to Final Video, Switch to Images, Update to Latest Video, Generate Video, Jump to Shot).
   - Right-clicking a clip inside a pinned group still shows only asset-state actions per the recent change.
   - The `usePinnedGroupSync` debounce still keeps the group's children in sync with the underlying Shot's `images[]`.
   - Final video and image modes still work.
   - Auto-shift on resize from the recent megaplan still works (or is replaced by a better mechanism in the new model).

3. **Persistence stays compatible.** The on-disk timeline config schema currently stores `pinnedShotGroups: { shotId, trackId, clipIds, mode, imageClipSnapshot? }[]`. Decide whether the new model can keep this on-disk shape (with internal restructuring), or whether a schema migration is needed. If a migration is needed, it must be backward-compatible (old configs load correctly).

4. **Remotion preview rendering stays correct.** The Remotion compositions render flat clips. The refactor must not break what Remotion sees — i.e., individual clips inside a group must still be addressable for rendering, even if the editing model treats them as children of a group.

5. **Tests pass.** All existing tests in `src/tools/video-editor/` must continue to pass, OR be updated with clear justification when behavior intentionally changes. New tests should cover the cohesion-by-construction property.

## Design questions the plan must answer

The planning phase needs to investigate the codebase and propose answers to all of these. Do not guess — read the code:

1. **Data model.** Which of these is the right abstraction?
   a) "Group action" embedded in `row.actions`: the row's actions array becomes a heterogeneous list of `TimelineAction | GroupAction`, where `GroupAction` has its own start/end and a `children[]` of TimelineActions. Drag/resize see the GroupAction as a single thing.
   b) "Group resolver" overlay: keep `row.actions` as flat TimelineActions, but introduce a `GroupResolver` that, given a clipId or set of clipIds, returns the group bounds and member list. Drag/resize hooks consult the resolver and operate on the whole group when any member is touched.
   c) Something else.
   Trade off: (a) is conceptually cleaner but ripples through many types and Remotion assumptions; (b) is less invasive but still leaves cohesion as a soft property enforced by callers.

2. **Selection model.** When the user clicks one clip inside a group, do they select (i) the clip, (ii) the group, or (iii) is there a hover/click distinction (e.g., one click = group, double click = clip)? The current behavior is "click selects the clip"; the recent context-menu change limits what that clip can do. Pick a model that makes "you cannot accidentally break a group by editing one of its clips" obvious.

3. **Internal layout.** Inside a group, children are positioned. Are positions stored absolutely (each child has its own row-relative start/end) or relatively (each child has an offset from the group's start)? Relative positioning makes "shift the whole group by N seconds" trivial and impossible to break; absolute positioning means children can drift relative to each other (the original bug). Strongly prefer relative.

4. **Interior boundaries.** Today, a user can resize the boundary between two child clips of a group via the auto-shift mechanism we added. Decide whether interior boundary resize is still allowed in the new model. If yes, define it precisely. If no, document why not (likely it should not be — the user already said they don't really want per-clip operations inside a group).

5. **Sync interplay.** `usePinnedGroupSync` watches the Shot's `images[]` and rewrites the group's clips. In the new model, this becomes "rewrite the children of the group in place." Specify how this interacts with in-progress drags/resizes (it currently debounces; does that still work?).

6. **Migration / persistence.** Define the on-disk shape and the load-time conversion if needed. Old configs must load.

7. **Snap & timeline-start clamp.** Today the bug-fix from a few minutes ago lets `findBestGroupStart` clamp the group to t≥0 as a unit. In the new model, the group's `start` would be clamped directly. Make sure that still happens in every drag/resize path.

8. **Reuse of existing infrastructure.** `multi-drag-utils.ts`, `resolve-overlaps.ts`, `useClipDrag.ts`, `useClipResize.ts`, `usePinnedShotGroups.ts`, and `useShotGroups.ts` all encode pieces of cohesion. Decide which become obsolete and which get repurposed. Avoid leaving dead code.

## Constraints

- Scope is the video-editor tool only. No DB schema changes, no edge functions, no migrations to Supabase. (The on-disk config is a JSON blob in the user's project file.)
- Follow the rules in CLAUDE.md at the repo root and in /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/CLAUDE.md.
- Prefer Edit over Write. No new docs unless explicitly necessary.
- Don't introduce new state management libraries or routing.
- Don't bundle unrelated cleanups. Keep the diff focused on the shot-group refactor.
- Pre-existing failing tests (one in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx about data-shot-group-kind="pinned") are unrelated and should be left alone.
- Reuse existing UI primitives in src/shared/components/ui/. No new color tokens.

## What success looks like

1. After the refactor, manually attempting to break a pinned shot group via any combination of normal drag, resize, snap, multi-select, or cross-track drag is impossible. The only way to break a group is the explicit Deconstruct/Delete actions.
2. Type check passes (npx tsc --noEmit).
3. Existing video-editor tests pass, plus new tests covering the cohesion-by-construction property.
4. The Remotion preview still renders the same output for an unchanged timeline.
5. The diff is concentrated in the video-editor tool with a clear before/after for the new abstraction.

    "CRITICAL LOCATION DECISION (gate override): the shared `interactionStateRef` is a LOCAL `useRef` in `TimelineEditor.tsx`, passed via props/callbacks to `useClipDrag`, `TimelineCanvas`, and `usePinnedGroupSync`. Do NOT lift it into `useTimelineState.contexts.ts` or the split context surface \u2014 that would require updating `useTimelineState.types.ts`, `TimelineEditorContext.tsx`, `VideoEditorProvider.tsx`, and `VideoEditorProvider.test.tsx`, which the gate explicitly rejected as unnecessary scope.",
    "Commit-boundary projection must run in TWO places: `buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` (before `resolvedConfig` is built) AND `useTimelineCommit.applyEdit` for types `'config'` and `'pinnedShotGroups'`. Missing either leaves a bypass.",
    "Keyboard Backspace/Delete and `s` (split) on group members are silent NO-OPs (error toast OK); they must NOT reroute to shot deletion. Only shot-label menu may break a group.",
    "ArrowUp/Down and `moveSelectedClipsToTrack` DO reroute \u2014 whole-group move via `moveGroup` \u2014 because moving preserves cohesion. Do not confuse with delete/split.",
    "`useExternalDrop` builds `children` inline from the loop's own `timeOffset` and `nextEdit.duration`; do NOT read `workingData.config.clips` (pre-commit clips not yet there).",
    "All three clone helpers (`migrate.ts:163`, `timeline-data.ts:91`, `usePinnedShotGroups.ts:31`) must deep-copy both `start` and `children`. Missing a clone site silently strips fields.",
    "`stableSignature` depends on projected config \u2014 projection MUST run before signature computation in `assembleTimelineData`'s caller chain.",
    "Delete shot menu preserves `handleDeleteClips(...)` then `unpinGroup(...)`; Unpin/Deconstruct is `unpinGroup` ONLY. Do not change.",
    "Sync-deferral: re-arm the debounce timer when `isInteractionActive()` is true; don't just skip (would starve the sync).",
    "`findBestGroupStart` remains the t>=0 clamp authority in every drag/resize path; `moveGroup` also clamps.",
    "`children[i].offset` is running cumulative; in `useExternalDrop` push BEFORE `timeOffset +=`.",
    "Pre-existing failing `data-shot-group-kind=\"pinned\"` TimelineCanvas test is unrelated \u2014 leave alone.",
    "Scope: video-editor tool only. No DB/edge/migration changes. No unrelated cleanups. Follow CLAUDE.md no-toast-on-success rule."

  "meta_commentary": "Execution order: Phase 1 (T1\u2192T2\u2192T4,T5\u2192T3\u2192T6) builds the foundation with projection and migration tested. T7 (local useRef in TimelineEditor.tsx, NOT context \u2014 this is the gate's explicit override) precedes T8/T9/T10 which depend on it. Phase 2 completes the write-path refactor: T8 (drag) \u2192 T9 (resize) \u2192 T10 (sync) \u2192 T11 (external drop) \u2192 T12 (edit surface) \u2192 T13 (menu verify). Phase 3 (T14,T15) cleans up UI and dead code. Phase 4 (T16,T17,T18) validates. Key judgment calls: (1) The interaction-state ref placement is the single most important execution decision \u2014 the plan text says `useTimelineState.contexts.ts` but the gate overrides this and mandates a local `useRef` in `TimelineEditor.tsx` passed via props. Follow the gate. (2) `useExternalDrop` must build children from loop state, not from `config.clips`, because freshly-dropped clips don't exist in config until after commit. Push `children` entries BEFORE incrementing `timeOffset` so offsets are the running cumulative. (3) Keyboard delete/split on group members are silent no-ops with an error toast \u2014 they do NOT reroute to shot-deletion semantics. ArrowUp/Down DO reroute because moving preserves cohesion. (4) Commit-boundary projection lives in two places (`buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` AND `applyEdit` for `'config'`/`'pinnedShotGroups'`); missing either leaves a bypass that will break `signature` consistency. (5) The pre-existing `data-shot-group-kind=\"pinned\"` TimelineCanvas test failure is unrelated per the brief \u2014 leave it alone. (6) Scope is strictly video-editor tool; no DB/edge/migration changes; no unrelated cleanups.",

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Refactor pinned shot groups in the video editor to be first-class timeline entities, eliminating an entire class of "shot group disconnects" bugs that come from fragile parallel cohesion logic spread across drag, resize, sync, and render code paths.

## Why

The video editor's pinned shot groups (the colored bordered boxes that visually group N timeline clips under a single shot label) are currently a "soft" abstraction layered on top of flat `row.actions` arrays. The shot group is just a `{ shotId, trackId, clipIds: string[] }` record stored in `data.config.pinnedShotGroups`. Cohesion (the property that all the group's clips stay contiguous on the timeline) is enforced by SEPARATE bespoke logic in every code path that mutates clip positions:

- Drag: `useClipDrag` does "deferred expansion" — when the user grabs one clip in a group, it pulls the group's other clips into the drag set. Then `multi-drag-utils.applyMultiDragMoves` calls `resolveGroupOverlaps` which treats moved clips as one shifted group.
- Resize: `TimelineCanvas` resize handlers compute a `getShotGroupAdjacent` neighbor and write a paired `ResizeOverride` so the immediate neighbor follows the dragged edge.
- Sync: `usePinnedGroupSync` runs on a debounce after the underlying Shot's `images[]` changes; it rebuilds the group's clips one-by-one and shifts subsequent clips on the row.
- Track move / cross-track drag: updates `pinnedShotGroups[].trackId` via `updatePinnedShotGroupTrackIds`.
- Delete / unpin / "deconstruct" / "delete shot": each touches `pinnedShotGroups` differently.

There's no shared "the group is the unit of editing" primitive. Each path reinvents cohesion. Bugs keep emerging in different paths with the same user-visible symptom: "the shot disconnects."

On top of that, the visual contiguity check in `TimelineCanvas.tsx` (`positionedShotGroups`, around lines 446-499) iterates `group.clipIds` IN ARRAY ORDER and checks `current.start < previous.start || current.start - previous.end > snapThreshold`. **This assumes `group.clipIds` is in left-to-right spatial order, but nothing actively maintains that invariant.** `pinGroup` stores `[...selectedClipIds]` (Set iteration order, i.e. click order), and `usePinnedGroupSync` rebuilds in `desiredImages` order from the database. Once array order disagrees with spatial order by even one position, the group's border vanishes — user reports "disconnected."

We just shipped two patches for this in the drag and resize paths. The patches work for their cases but they don't address the root cause: the shot group is not a real entity in the editing model.

## Goal

Make a pinned shot group a FIRST-CLASS timeline entity. Conceptually:

- The shot group has its own bounds (`start`, `end`) on the row.
- It contains child clips with their own positions inside that bound (or relative offsets).
- Drag, resize, selection, and serialization all see the group as ONE thing by default.
- The contiguity invariant is enforced BY CONSTRUCTION, not by validation. Children of a group can never become non-contiguous because they live inside a single owning entity that lays them out.
- Operations on individual clips inside a group are explicit, intentional, and rare (the per-clip context menu is already locked down to asset-state actions only when a clip is in a pinned group, per a recent change).

## Hard requirements

1. **Eliminate the "shot disconnects" bug class.** After this refactor, no drag, resize, sync, or other normal interaction can make a pinned shot group's children become non-contiguous. The only way to break a group is the explicit "Deconstruct shot" or "Delete shot" actions on the shot label menu.

2. **No regression in user-facing behavior** for the supported flows:
   - Pinning a group from a selection.
   - Dragging a group on its row (snaps to bounds, snaps near siblings, can hit timeline start at t=0).
   - Dragging a group cross-track (entire group moves to the new row).
   - Resizing a group's outer left/right edge (stretches/shrinks the group, content reflows).
   - Resizing an interior boundary inside a group (between child clips) — re-evaluate whether this is even meaningful in the new model. If kept, must preserve cohesion.
   - The shot label menu actions (Deconstruct, Delete, Switch to Final Video, Switch to Images, Update to Latest Video, Generate Video, Jump to Shot).
   - Right-clicking a clip inside a pinned group still shows only asset-state actions per the recent change.
   - The `usePinnedGroupSync` debounce still keeps the group's children in sync with the underlying Shot's `images[]`.
   - Final video and image modes still work.
   - Auto-shift on resize from the recent megaplan still works (or is replaced by a better mechanism in the new model).

3. **Persistence stays compatible.** The on-disk timeline config schema currently stores `pinnedShotGroups: { shotId, trackId, clipIds, mode, imageClipSnapshot? }[]`. Decide whether the new model can keep this on-disk shape (with internal restructuring), or whether a schema migration is needed. If a migration is needed, it must be backward-compatible (old configs load correctly).

4. **Remotion preview rendering stays correct.** The Remotion compositions render flat clips. The refactor must not break what Remotion sees — i.e., individual clips inside a group must still be addressable for rendering, even if the editing model treats them as children of a group.

5. **Tests pass.** All existing tests in `src/tools/video-editor/` must continue to pass, OR be updated with clear justification when behavior intentionally changes. New tests should cover the cohesion-by-construction property.

## Design questions the plan must answer

The planning phase needs to investigate the codebase and propose answers to all of these. Do not guess — read the code:

1. **Data model.** Which of these is the right abstraction?
   a) "Group action" embedded in `row.actions`: the row's actions array becomes a heterogeneous list of `TimelineAction | GroupAction`, where `GroupAction` has its own start/end and a `children[]` of TimelineActions. Drag/resize see the GroupAction as a single thing.
   b) "Group resolver" overlay: keep `row.actions` as flat TimelineActions, but introduce a `GroupResolver` that, given a clipId or set of clipIds, returns the group bounds and member list. Drag/resize hooks consult the resolver and operate on the whole group when any member is touched.
   c) Something else.
   Trade off: (a) is conceptually cleaner but ripples through many types and Remotion assumptions; (b) is less invasive but still leaves cohesion as a soft property enforced by callers.

2. **Selection model.** When the user clicks one clip inside a group, do they select (i) the clip, (ii) the group, or (iii) is there a hover/click distinction (e.g., one click = group, double click = clip)? The current behavior is "click selects the clip"; the recent context-menu change limits what that clip can do. Pick a model that makes "you cannot accidentally break a group by editing one of its clips" obvious.

3. **Internal layout.** Inside a group, children are positioned. Are positions stored absolutely (each child has its own row-relative start/end) or relatively (each child has an offset from the group's start)? Relative positioning makes "shift the whole group by N seconds" trivial and impossible to break; absolute positioning means children can drift relative to each other (the original bug). Strongly prefer relative.

4. **Interior boundaries.** Today, a user can resize the boundary between two child clips of a group via the auto-shift mechanism we added. Decide whether interior boundary resize is still allowed in the new model. If yes, define it precisely. If no, document why not (likely it should not be — the user already said they don't really want per-clip operations inside a group).

5. **Sync interplay.** `usePinnedGroupSync` watches the Shot's `images[]` and rewrites the group's clips. In the new model, this becomes "rewrite the children of the group in place." Specify how this interacts with in-progress drags/resizes (it currently debounces; does that still work?).

6. **Migration / persistence.** Define the on-disk shape and the load-time conversion if needed. Old configs must load.

7. **Snap & timeline-start clamp.** Today the bug-fix from a few minutes ago lets `findBestGroupStart` clamp the group to t≥0 as a unit. In the new model, the group's `start` would be clamped directly. Make sure that still happens in every drag/resize path.

8. **Reuse of existing infrastructure.** `multi-drag-utils.ts`, `resolve-overlaps.ts`, `useClipDrag.ts`, `useClipResize.ts`, `usePinnedShotGroups.ts`, and `useShotGroups.ts` all encode pieces of cohesion. Decide which become obsolete and which get repurposed. Avoid leaving dead code.

## Files most likely to change

Start by reading these to ground the plan. Many more files will surface — explore from these as anchors:

- Data shape and config:
  - src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts (look for PinnedShotGroup, TimelineConfig)
  - src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts (resolve config -> TimelineData)
- Group accessors and sync:
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useShotGroups.ts
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts (pinGroup, unpinGroup, updatePinnedGroup, usePinnedGroupSync)
- Drag:

## Constraints

- Scope is the video-editor tool only. No DB schema changes, no edge functions, no migrations to Supabase. (The on-disk config is a JSON blob in the user's project file.)
- Follow the rules in CLAUDE.md at the repo root and in /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/CLAUDE.md.
- Prefer Edit over Write. No new docs unless explicitly necessary.
- Don't introduce new state management libraries or routing.
- Don't bundle unrelated cleanups. Keep the diff focused on the shot-group refactor.
- Pre-existing failing tests (one in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx about data-shot-group-kind="pinned") are unrelated and should be left alone.
- Reuse existing UI primitives in src/shared/components/ui/. No new color tokens.

## What success looks like

1. After the refactor, manually attempting to break a pinned shot group via any combination of normal drag, resize, snap, multi-select, or cross-track drag is impossible. The only way to break a group is the explicit Deconstruct/Delete actions.
2. Type check passes (npx tsc --noEmit).
3. Existing video-editor tests pass, plus new tests covering the cohesion-by-construction property.
4. The Remotion preview still renders the same output for an unchanged timeline.
5. The diff is concentrated in the video-editor tool with a clear before/after for the new abstraction.

    "CRITICAL LOCATION DECISION (gate override): the shared `interactionStateRef` is a LOCAL `useRef` in `TimelineEditor.tsx`, passed via props/callbacks to `useClipDrag`, `TimelineCanvas`, and `usePinnedGroupSync`. Do NOT lift it into `useTimelineState.contexts.ts` or the split context surface \u2014 that would require updating `useTimelineState.types.ts`, `TimelineEditorContext.tsx`, `VideoEditorProvider.tsx`, and `VideoEditorProvider.test.tsx`, which the gate explicitly rejected as unnecessary scope.",
    "Commit-boundary projection must run in TWO places: `buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` (before `resolvedConfig` is built) AND `useTimelineCommit.applyEdit` for types `'config'` and `'pinnedShotGroups'`. Missing either leaves a bypass.",
    "Keyboard Backspace/Delete and `s` (split) on group members are silent NO-OPs (error toast OK); they must NOT reroute to shot deletion. Only shot-label menu may break a group.",
    "ArrowUp/Down and `moveSelectedClipsToTrack` DO reroute \u2014 whole-group move via `moveGroup` \u2014 because moving preserves cohesion. Do not confuse with delete/split.",
    "`useExternalDrop` builds `children` inline from the loop's own `timeOffset` and `nextEdit.duration`; do NOT read `workingData.config.clips` (pre-commit clips not yet there).",
    "All three clone helpers (`migrate.ts:163`, `timeline-data.ts:91`, `usePinnedShotGroups.ts:31`) must deep-copy both `start` and `children`. Missing a clone site silently strips fields.",
    "`stableSignature` depends on projected config \u2014 projection MUST run before signature computation in `assembleTimelineData`'s caller chain.",
    "Delete shot menu preserves `handleDeleteClips(...)` then `unpinGroup(...)`; Unpin/Deconstruct is `unpinGroup` ONLY. Do not change.",
    "Sync-deferral: re-arm the debounce timer when `isInteractionActive()` is true; don't just skip (would starve the sync).",
    "`findBestGroupStart` remains the t>=0 clamp authority in every drag/resize path; `moveGroup` also clamps.",
    "`children[i].offset` is running cumulative; in `useExternalDrop` push BEFORE `timeOffset +=`.",
    "Pre-existing failing `data-shot-group-kind=\"pinned\"` TimelineCanvas test is unrelated \u2014 leave alone.",
    "Scope: video-editor tool only. No DB/edge/migration changes. No unrelated cleanups. Follow CLAUDE.md no-toast-on-success rule."

  "meta_commentary": "Execution order: Phase 1 (T1\u2192T2\u2192T4,T5\u2192T3\u2192T6) builds the foundation with projection and migration tested. T7 (local useRef in TimelineEditor.tsx, NOT context \u2014 this is the gate's explicit override) precedes T8/T9/T10 which depend on it. Phase 2 completes the write-path refactor: T8 (drag) \u2192 T9 (resize) \u2192 T10 (sync) \u2192 T11 (external drop) \u2192 T12 (edit surface) \u2192 T13 (menu verify). Phase 3 (T14,T15) cleans up UI and dead code. Phase 4 (T16,T17,T18) validates. Key judgment calls: (1) The interaction-state ref placement is the single most important execution decision \u2014 the plan text says `useTimelineState.contexts.ts` but the gate overrides this and mandates a local `useRef` in `TimelineEditor.tsx` passed via props. Follow the gate. (2) `useExternalDrop` must build children from loop state, not from `config.clips`, because freshly-dropped clips don't exist in config until after commit. Push `children` entries BEFORE incrementing `timeOffset` so offsets are the running cumulative. (3) Keyboard delete/split on group members are silent no-ops with an error toast \u2014 they do NOT reroute to shot-deletion semantics. ArrowUp/Down DO reroute because moving preserves cohesion. (4) Commit-boundary projection lives in two places (`buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` AND `applyEdit` for `'config'`/`'pinnedShotGroups'`); missing either leaves a bypass that will break `signature` consistency. (5) The pre-existing `data-shot-group-kind=\"pinned\"` TimelineCanvas test failure is unrelated per the brief \u2014 leave it alone. (6) Scope is strictly video-editor tool; no DB/edge/migration changes; no unrelated cleanups.",

        Idea:
Refactor pinned shot groups in the video editor to be first-class timeline entities, eliminating an entire class of "shot group disconnects" bugs that come from fragile parallel cohesion logic spread across drag, resize, sync, and render code paths.

## Why

The video editor's pinned shot groups (the colored bordered boxes that visually group N timeline clips under a single shot label) are currently a "soft" abstraction layered on top of flat `row.actions` arrays. The shot group is just a `{ shotId, trackId, clipIds: string[] }` record stored in `data.config.pinnedShotGroups`. Cohesion (the property that all the group's clips stay contiguous on the timeline) is enforced by SEPARATE bespoke logic in every code path that mutates clip positions:

- Drag: `useClipDrag` does "deferred expansion" — when the user grabs one clip in a group, it pulls the group's other clips into the drag set. Then `multi-drag-utils.applyMultiDragMoves` calls `resolveGroupOverlaps` which treats moved clips as one shifted group.
- Resize: `TimelineCanvas` resize handlers compute a `getShotGroupAdjacent` neighbor and write a paired `ResizeOverride` so the immediate neighbor follows the dragged edge.
- Sync: `usePinnedGroupSync` runs on a debounce after the underlying Shot's `images[]` changes; it rebuilds the group's clips one-by-one and shifts subsequent clips on the row.
- Track move / cross-track drag: updates `pinnedShotGroups[].trackId` via `updatePinnedShotGroupTrackIds`.
- Delete / unpin / "deconstruct" / "delete shot": each touches `pinnedShotGroups` differently.

There's no shared "the group is the unit of editing" primitive. Each path reinvents cohesion. Bugs keep emerging in different paths with the same user-visible symptom: "the shot disconnects."

On top of that, the visual contiguity check in `TimelineCanvas.tsx` (`positionedShotGroups`, around lines 446-499) iterates `group.clipIds` IN ARRAY ORDER and checks `current.start < previous.start || current.start - previous.end > snapThreshold`. **This assumes `group.clipIds` is in left-to-right spatial order, but nothing actively maintains that invariant.** `pinGroup` stores `[...selectedClipIds]` (Set iteration order, i.e. click order), and `usePinnedGroupSync` rebuilds in `desiredImages` order from the database. Once array order disagrees with spatial order by even one position, the group's border vanishes — user reports "disconnected."

We just shipped two patches for this in the drag and resize paths. The patches work for their cases but they don't address the root cause: the shot group is not a real entity in the editing model.

## Goal

Make a pinned shot group a FIRST-CLASS timeline entity. Conceptually:

- The shot group has its own bounds (`start`, `end`) on the row.
- It contains child clips with their own positions inside that bound (or relative offsets).
- Drag, resize, selection, and serialization all see the group as ONE thing by default.
- The contiguity invariant is enforced BY CONSTRUCTION, not by validation. Children of a group can never become non-contiguous because they live inside a single owning entity that lays them out.
- Operations on individual clips inside a group are explicit, intentional, and rare (the per-clip context menu is already locked down to asset-state actions only when a clip is in a pinned group, per a recent change).

## Hard requirements

1. **Eliminate the "shot disconnects" bug class.** After this refactor, no drag, resize, sync, or other normal interaction can make a pinned shot group's children become non-contiguous. The only way to break a group is the explicit "Deconstruct shot" or "Delete shot" actions on the shot label menu.

2. **No regression in user-facing behavior** for the supported flows:
   - Pinning a group from a selection.
   - Dragging a group on its row (snaps to bounds, snaps near siblings, can hit timeline start at t=0).
   - Dragging a group cross-track (entire group moves to the new row).
   - Resizing a group's outer left/right edge (stretches/shrinks the group, content reflows).
   - Resizing an interior boundary inside a group (between child clips) — re-evaluate whether this is even meaningful in the new model. If kept, must preserve cohesion.
   - The shot label menu actions (Deconstruct, Delete, Switch to Final Video, Switch to Images, Update to Latest Video, Generate Video, Jump to Shot).
   - Right-clicking a clip inside a pinned group still shows only asset-state actions per the recent change.
   - The `usePinnedGroupSync` debounce still keeps the group's children in sync with the underlying Shot's `images[]`.
   - Final video and image modes still work.
   - Auto-shift on resize from the recent megaplan still works (or is replaced by a better mechanism in the new model).

3. **Persistence stays compatible.** The on-disk timeline config schema currently stores `pinnedShotGroups: { shotId, trackId, clipIds, mode, imageClipSnapshot? }[]`. Decide whether the new model can keep this on-disk shape (with internal restructuring), or whether a schema migration is needed. If a migration is needed, it must be backward-compatible (old configs load correctly).

4. **Remotion preview rendering stays correct.** The Remotion compositions render flat clips. The refactor must not break what Remotion sees — i.e., individual clips inside a group must still be addressable for rendering, even if the editing model treats them as children of a group.

5. **Tests pass.** All existing tests in `src/tools/video-editor/` must continue to pass, OR be updated with clear justification when behavior intentionally changes. New tests should cover the cohesion-by-construction property.

## Design questions the plan must answer

The planning phase needs to investigate the codebase and propose answers to all of these. Do not guess — read the code:

1. **Data model.** Which of these is the right abstraction?
   a) "Group action" embedded in `row.actions`: the row's actions array becomes a heterogeneous list of `TimelineAction | GroupAction`, where `GroupAction` has its own start/end and a `children[]` of TimelineActions. Drag/resize see the GroupAction as a single thing.
   b) "Group resolver" overlay: keep `row.actions` as flat TimelineActions, but introduce a `GroupResolver` that, given a clipId or set of clipIds, returns the group bounds and member list. Drag/resize hooks consult the resolver and operate on the whole group when any member is touched.
   c) Something else.
   Trade off: (a) is conceptually cleaner but ripples through many types and Remotion assumptions; (b) is less invasive but still leaves cohesion as a soft property enforced by callers.

2. **Selection model.** When the user clicks one clip inside a group, do they select (i) the clip, (ii) the group, or (iii) is there a hover/click distinction (e.g., one click = group, double click = clip)? The current behavior is "click selects the clip"; the recent context-menu change limits what that clip can do. Pick a model that makes "you cannot accidentally break a group by editing one of its clips" obvious.

3. **Internal layout.** Inside a group, children are positioned. Are positions stored absolutely (each child has its own row-relative start/end) or relatively (each child has an offset from the group's start)? Relative positioning makes "shift the whole group by N seconds" trivial and impossible to break; absolute positioning means children can drift relative to each other (the original bug). Strongly prefer relative.

4. **Interior boundaries.** Today, a user can resize the boundary between two child clips of a group via the auto-shift mechanism we added. Decide whether interior boundary resize is still allowed in the new model. If yes, define it precisely. If no, document why not (likely it should not be — the user already said they don't really want per-clip operations inside a group).

5. **Sync interplay.** `usePinnedGroupSync` watches the Shot's `images[]` and rewrites the group's clips. In the new model, this becomes "rewrite the children of the group in place." Specify how this interacts with in-progress drags/resizes (it currently debounces; does that still work?).

6. **Migration / persistence.** Define the on-disk shape and the load-time conversion if needed. Old configs must load.

7. **Snap & timeline-start clamp.** Today the bug-fix from a few minutes ago lets `findBestGroupStart` clamp the group to t≥0 as a unit. In the new model, the group's `start` would be clamped directly. Make sure that still happens in every drag/resize path.

8. **Reuse of existing infrastructure.** `multi-drag-utils.ts`, `resolve-overlaps.ts`, `useClipDrag.ts`, `useClipResize.ts`, `usePinnedShotGroups.ts`, and `useShotGroups.ts` all encode pieces of cohesion. Decide which become obsolete and which get repurposed. Avoid leaving dead code.

## Files most likely to change

Start by reading these to ground the plan. Many more files will surface — explore from these as anchors:

- Data shape and config:
  - src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts (look for PinnedShotGroup, TimelineConfig)
  - src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts (resolve config -> TimelineData)
- Group accessors and sync:
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useShotGroups.ts
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts (pinGroup, unpinGroup, updatePinnedGroup, usePinnedGroupSync)
- Drag:
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts (expandPinnedGroupDrag, deferredPinnedGroupClipIds)
  - src/tools/video-editor/lib/multi-drag-utils.ts (planMultiDragMoves, applyMultiDragMoves, updatePinnedShotGroupTrackIds)
  - src/tools/video-editor/lib/resolve-overlaps.ts (resolveGroupOverlaps, findBestGroupStart)
  - src/tools/video-editor/lib/snap-edges.ts
- Resize:
  - src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx (resize handlers, getShotGroupAdjacent, positionedShotGroups)
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.ts (auto-shift transaction logic from the recent megaplan)
- Render and editor wiring:
  - src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx
  - src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx
  - src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ShotGroupContextMenu.tsx
- Provider / state:
  - src/tools/video-editor/contexts/VideoEditorProvider.tsx
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.types.ts
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.contexts.ts
- Final video / image mode switching:
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts (or similarly named)
- Remotion / preview:
  - Whatever in src/tools/video-editor/compositions/ and src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/ reads the timeline config -- verify it does not depend on flat row.actions in a way the new model breaks.

## Constraints

- Scope is the video-editor tool only. No DB schema changes, no edge functions, no migrations to Supabase. (The on-disk config is a JSON blob in the user's project file.)
- Follow the rules in CLAUDE.md at the repo root and in /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/CLAUDE.md.
- Prefer Edit over Write. No new docs unless explicitly necessary.
- Don't introduce new state management libraries or routing.
- Don't bundle unrelated cleanups. Keep the diff focused on the shot-group refactor.
- Pre-existing failing tests (one in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx about data-shot-group-kind="pinned") are unrelated and should be left alone.
- Reuse existing UI primitives in src/shared/components/ui/. No new color tokens.

## What success looks like

1. After the refactor, manually attempting to break a pinned shot group via any combination of normal drag, resize, snap, multi-select, or cross-track drag is impossible. The only way to break a group is the explicit Deconstruct/Delete actions.
2. Type check passes (npx tsc --noEmit).
3. Existing video-editor tests pass, plus new tests covering the cohesion-by-construction property.
4. The Remotion preview still renders the same output for an unchanged timeline.
5. The diff is concentrated in the video-editor tool with a clear before/after for the new abstraction.

  "meta_commentary": "Execution order: Phase 1 (T1\u2192T2\u2192T4,T5\u2192T3\u2192T6) builds the foundation with projection and migration tested. T7 (local useRef in TimelineEditor.tsx, NOT context \u2014 this is the gate's explicit override) precedes T8/T9/T10 which depend on it. Phase 2 completes the write-path refactor: T8 (drag) \u2192 T9 (resize) \u2192 T10 (sync) \u2192 T11 (external drop) \u2192 T12 (edit surface) \u2192 T13 (menu verify). Phase 3 (T14,T15) cleans up UI and dead code. Phase 4 (T16,T17,T18) validates. Key judgment calls: (1) The interaction-state ref placement is the single most important execution decision \u2014 the plan text says `useTimelineState.contexts.ts` but the gate overrides this and mandates a local `useRef` in `TimelineEditor.tsx` passed via props. Follow the gate. (2) `useExternalDrop` must build children from loop state, not from `config.clips`, because freshly-dropped clips don't exist in config until after commit. Push `children` entries BEFORE incrementing `timeOffset` so offsets are the running cumulative. (3) Keyboard delete/split on group members are silent no-ops with an error toast \u2014 they do NOT reroute to shot-deletion semantics. ArrowUp/Down DO reroute because moving preserves cohesion. (4) Commit-boundary projection lives in two places (`buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` AND `applyEdit` for `'config'`/`'pinnedShotGroups'`); missing either leaves a bypass that will break `signature` consistency. (5) The pre-existing `data-shot-group-kind=\"pinned\"` TimelineCanvas test failure is unrelated per the brief \u2014 leave it alone. (6) Scope is strictly video-editor tool; no DB/edge/migration changes; no unrelated cleanups.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 2.4 against `materializedata()` in `usetimelinecommit` and `assembletimelinedata()` in `timeline-data.ts`. the plan still says to overwrite `pinnedshotgroups` after `materializedata()` returns, but `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so mutating the config afterward would leave signature-derived state stale unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the grouped-drag plan against `useclipdrag` and `usetimelinetrackmanagement.movecliptorow()`. the plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still does not say how `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` is updated when a grouped drag or move lands on a different row, so the overlay and menu state can still point at the old track after a successful cross-track move. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the cross-track multi-drag new-track branch in `useclipdrag`. that path still commits through `applyedit({ type: 'config', resolvedconfig })` using `buildconfigfromdragresult()`, and the revised plan does not explain how pinned-group metadata is updated atomically on that path, so grouped drags that create a new track are still under-specified even beyond the stale-`trackid` problem. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed shared interaction-state location against the actual hook graph in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:194`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.ts:172-204`, and `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.contexts.ts:72-166`. rev 3 says the ref should live in `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts`, but that file only memoizes context values while `useclipdrag`, `timelinecanvas`, and `usepinnedgroupsync` are all coordinated from `timelineeditor`, so the stated location and rationale are still technically off. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: cross-track group motion is broader than step 18 currently states. because the pinned-group model now includes `trackid`, every move path that can relocate a grouped clip set across rows has to update pinned-group metadata too; otherwise the overlay and menu logic will still reference the previous track even though the clip actions moved successfully. - [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: if phases 6 and 7 are deferred, the shipped work becomes a narrower "pin existing inferred groups and toggle them" feature rather than the full brief, which also called out shot-drop creation and shot-image auto-sync. that broader scope tension is still present in the revised plan. - [DEBT] task-deduplication: server-generated idempotency keys would break retry deduplication because retried requests get fresh keys. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
